# handsoff — hardware acceptance checklist

Run this on the real desktop (Arch/CachyOS + niri + Yeti mic + speakers)
after any deployment, and especially after installer, audio, or
desktop-action changes. Every line is meant to be executed, not read.
Nothing here can be fully automated — that is what `tests/` is for.

Legend: ☐ pending · ✅ pass · ❌ fail (note why below the line)
Verified-by codes: [auto] scriptable end-to-end on this machine ·
[human] needs ears/eyes/hands at the desk.

## 1. Deployment trust (P0)

- ✅ [auto] `./install.sh` completes; `~/.config/handsoff/deployment.json`
  exists with a row for **every** shipped file — the five top-level entries
  (`handsoff.py`, `handsoff-settings.py`, `handsoff-restart`,
  `settings_schema.py`, `hardware.py`) plus every `core/*.py` module, all
  `source==installed: True`. The core set is generated by glob over the
  staged tree, so a newly added module cannot be left behind (re-verified
  2026-09-11: 15 rows, `core/theme.py` among them).
  Re-verified 2026-09-09
  after the ff2f158 run (per-package pacman probe + unit-name loop fired live).
- ✅ [auto] `--ptt doctor` prints
  `deployment: in-sync — installed copy matches the checkout` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] `--ptt health` includes a `"deployment"` section with
  `status: in-sync` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Settings health bar shows `deploy: ok` — verified at the data
  layer: the live bubble health snapshot fed through the Settings app's own
  `_fmt_health()` renders
  `mic: … · brain: ok gemma4:latest · tts/stt: ok · deploy: ok`
  (the string the bar displays verbatim — `gemma4:latest` was the
  configured value on the test box; the shipped default is `qwen3:8b`; pixel-OCR of the Qt window was
  attempted but multi-output screenshots made OCR unreliable)
- ✅ [auto] `systemctl --user status handsoff` → active; unit has
  `Restart=always` (2026-09-09)

## 2. Startup, restart, crash recovery, shutdown (P0)

- ✅ [auto] Fresh start: stop → `systemctl --user start handsoff` → unit
  active, `--ptt status` answers `state=idle handsfree=off model=gemma4:latest`
  (`gemma4:latest` was the configured value on the test box; default `qwen3:8b`)
  (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Restart: `~/.local/bin/handsoff-restart` → exactly one bubble
  after the swap. **Found and fixed a real race today**: against a
  systemd-owned bubble the script's manual TERM/KILL pass triggered
  `Restart=always` auto-spawn before its own `systemctl restart` (transient
  double bubble, journal 16:39:13–16:39:15). The script now goes systemd-first
  and only kills manually when no unit is managing the bubble. Retest: PASS.
- ✅ [auto] Crash recovery: SEGV on the main pid → systemd restarts within
  ~5 s; doctor mentions the crash log; previous run (15:08–15:09) additionally
  showed the spoken short report and `crash.log` truncated-not-unlinked,
  same inode, mode 0600.
- ✅ [auto] Crash-loop guard: 5+ rapid SEGVs → unit entered `failed` with
  journal `Start request repeated too quickly`; `systemctl --user reset-failed
  handsoff` + start recovered it to active (2026-09-09)
- ☐ [human] Shutdown: end the graphical session → bubble exits cleanly
  (`PartOf=graphical-session.target`), no zombie after re-login. Not
  automatable from inside the session (it *is* the session).

## 3. Yeti capture (P0/P1)

- ☐ [human+auto] Settings → Voice: the Yeti appears in *Input device* and can
  be selected. Enumeration verified 2026-09-09: `Blue Microphones: USB Audio
  (hw:4,0)` is visible to sounddevice (Blue = Yeti vendor), **but the
  currently configured input is the Logitech StreamCam** — the Yeti needs to
  be actually selected in Settings → Voice.
- ☐ [human] Live test: level meter follows the room; speech passes threshold
- ☐ [human] Push-to-talk round trip: hold → speak → release; transcript correct
- ☐ [human] Hands-free wake: say the wake name; engagement window opens
- ☐ [human] Stop echo rejection: while it speaks a long reply, say "stop" →
  quiet, no self-transcription
- ☐ [human] Mic self-heal: unplug the Yeti hands-free → degraded state
  journaled/spoken, recovers on replug (or capped retries)

## 4. TTS (P0/P1)

- ☐ [human, synthesis verified] Replies spoken with the configured voice; no
  echo into the transcript. Engine verified 2026-09-12 on chatterbox-turbo:
  `tts_to_wav` writes 24 kHz 16-bit PCM, and with a reference clip set the
  conditionals provably change the audio (same sentence, clone vs built-in:
  waveform correlation +0.015, f0 138 Hz vs 218 Hz against the clip's 110 Hz).
  Audible playback + echo-rejection need ears.
- ☐ [human] Barge-in: click the bubble while it speaks → playback stops
  instantly (needs the bubble audibly speaking; supervised test)
- ☐ [human] Volume/rate sliders in Settings take effect after Apply

## 5. ydotool / typing (P1)

One-pass re-check: `python ~/.local/bin/handsoff.py --ptt selftest` runs the
whole section (daemon, focus plumbing, terminal refusal, type_text landing,
clipboard round-trip) against self-launched scratch windows and restores the
clipboard. First live run 2026-09-09: `verdict: PASS (5/5 checks)`.

- ✅ [auto] ydotoold running (Arch user unit: `ydotool.service`; other
  distros: `ydotoold.service`). Verified 2026-09-09: daemon reachable at
  `/run/user/1000/.ydotool_socket`, doctor reports
  `ydotool: ok (daemon reachable …)`. The installer now enables the right
  unit (`ydotool.service` first) — confirmed in the ff2f158 install run.
- ✅ [auto] Type into a focused editor → text lands in the app (2026-09-09):
  `type_text "handsoff ydotool e2e 2026-09-09"` into gnome-text-editor →
  "typed 31 chars into org.gnome.TextEditor"; full-screen OCR read the exact
  string back off the screen.
- ✅ [auto] Typing into a *focused terminal* is refused (2026-09-09): foot
  focused → `type_text` AND `press_keys` both returned
  "REFUSED: the focused window is a terminal (foot)"; nothing was executed.
  Fail-closed on unknown focus is unit-tested in tests/test_desktop.py.
- ✅ [auto] `press_keys "ctrl+c"` copies from the focused app (2026-09-09):
  ctrl+a then ctrl+c in gnome-text-editor → `wl-paste` returned the exact
  file content byte-for-byte.

## 6. niri IPC & desktop actions (P1)

- ✅ [auto] `run_command "niri msg --json windows"` works (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] `niri_capabilities` lists this niri version's actions, not a
  hardcoded set (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] open_app launches, identifies and focuses: `open_app foot` →
  window appeared, `is_focused: true` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Move to workspace: `workspace move "texteditor to 2"` →
  `moved the window to workspace 2`; window verified on workspace idx 2.
  (Note: `workspace_id` in niri's JSON is a global id, NOT the user-facing
  index — verify against `niri msg --json workspaces` idx, as this run did.)
- ✅ [auto] Polite close confirmed gone: `close_window "texteditor"` →
  "closed 1 window(s): … (confirmed gone)", window absent afterwards
- ✅ [auto] scroll scrolls (`scrolled down 2 notch(es)` with the operator
  permission enabled the legitimate way — wheel via ydotool) and `wait`
  settles (2026-09-09)
- ☐ [human, supervised] Operator element clicks: `screen_elements` →
  `click_element` clicks the right element; pointer scale correct on a
  fractional-scale output. Left supervised: a mis-aimed click on the real
  desktop could hit unrelated windows.

## 7. systemd / niri autostart ownership (P0)

- ✅ [auto] Exactly one autostart owner: unit `enabled`, and no handsoff
  `spawn-at-startup` line in `~/.config/niri/config.kdl` (2026-09-09)
- ☐ [human] After a full reboot the bubble is up exactly once (needs a reboot)

## 8. Session teardown

- ☐ [human] After logout/login, reminders and hands-free state match
  expectations
- ✅ [auto] `~/.local/state/handsoff/decisions.jsonl` exists (mode 0600,
  909 rows) and every row carries id, tool, target, decision, result —
  including all of this session's tool calls (2026-09-09)

Two real traps were found by measuring: the window carries an **inscribed-ellipse mask**, and the first ear geometry leaked **818 px** outside it in the LISTENING state (where the radius grows to only ~1.12 R of headroom) — retuned to 0 px across every state/level/anim combination; and **`grab()` is not a valid instrument on a translucent widget** (it reported 1753 px of overflow on `bloom` that vanished when rendered onto a cleared image, because `WA_TranslucentBackground` means the background is never erased). The overflow sweep now renders onto a cleared `QImage` and reads alpha, which also exposed two pre-existing overflows in that same state (`droplet` 251 px, `sauron` 305 px) recorded rather than silently "fixed". (2) **Settings → Voice has a live level meter** fed by a new control-socket `level` command: `Assistant._emit_level(value, source)` is now the single publisher all three producers call (`mic` = continuous listener, `ptt` = push-to-talk, `tts` = playback), and `level_snapshot()` returns `raw`, the smoothed `ui` the painters read, `source`, `age_s` (which advances only on a non-zero level, so a wedged zero-emitting feed is visible), `state` and `handsfree`. The meter polls on its own daemon thread and only while its tab is on screen (`doctor`/`health` hold the control server's accept loop, so `level` is answered inline and those two stay off-thread). Verified live against the running bubble via the meter's own code path: idle → `{'raw': 0.0, 'source': 'tts', 'age_s': 13.7, …}`; `--ptt level` returns the same JSON; then making the bubble speak produced **19 non-zero samples, max raw 0.59 / max designs 0.54, source `tts`**. Guards were verified to fail on mutated code (removing the `level` branch ⇒ JSONDecodeError; folding `ui` into `raw` ⇒ AssertionError, driving the real window against a real unix socket). Deployed (`in-sync`, three-way TRUE, 15/15 files); 772 tests green, coverage 75.42% ≥ 70, `ci/compile_all.py` clean; human-only items unchanged |
| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (live redeploy) | uncommitted second-review-batch set (running == checkout == installed, handsoff.py sha256 26e19b95…) | A second "major" list (items 7–28) was read against the tree before any edit: **nine real and fixed, one false, twelve not examined and listed as open in `GAP_ANALYSIS.md`.** Fixed: (7) a failed settings write still updated the in-memory copy *and* re-stamped the version and refreshed the derived globals, so the runtime used a value the next start would not read back — `_persist_setting` now returns a bool and callers keep the old value on failure. (8) backup sidecars were never hardened — `shutil.copy2` preserves the *source* mode, and this machine really had `history.json.bak-modelswitch` (a full transcript) at **0644** along with four other `.bak-*` files; new backups are forced to 0600 and `_secure_runtime_files()` now sweeps `*.bak*` on startup — after redeploy **all five loose sidecars are `-rw-------`**. (11) `play_wav` built its stream outside the `try`, so a failure skipped the final `_emit_level(0.0)` (designs frozen at the last level) and leaked the stream; both are in the `finally` now. (12) `tts_to_wav` truncated the target before synthesizing, so a failure left a garbage wav — it now writes a `.part<pid>` sibling and atomically replaces. (13) `Recorder.start()` leaked the device on a double start and `_cb`/`stop()` raced on `_frames`; a buffer lock fixes it and is deliberately *not* held across `stream.stop()` (PortAudio joins the callback there — that would deadlock). (15) the native-rate retry discarded the original error; it is chained now. (16) `strip_thinking` missed *unclosed* `<think>` blocks (reasoning spoken aloud) while the stream loop dropped any sentence merely starting with `<`, silently eating legitimate replies like "<3" — the filter is now a closed control-token set, and the duplicated fallback filter was hoisted to one implementation with a drift test. (17) after a barge-in the buffered tail was queued anyway and spoken over the user. (18) `bool("false")` was `True` (flag inverted), junk became 0, and — unlisted in the finding — an **omitted optional numeric argument was sent as 0 instead of taking its documented default**, making `snooze_reminder(minutes=10)`'s default unreachable; coercion is strict and missing defaults now apply. **Not reproducible:** (10) a dead SOCK_DGRAM server gives `ECONNREFUSED (111)` on `connect()`, so `ydotoold` liveness was never falsely reported — and the proposed `/proc` inode check would have broken a root-owned daemon. Every guard was verified to fail on the mutated code. Deployed (`in-sync`, three-way TRUE, service active; installed copies carry each fix); 816 tests green, coverage 75.8% ≥ 70, `ci/compile_all.py` clean; human-only items unchanged |
| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (live redeploy) | uncommitted six-item critical-review set (running == checkout == installed, handsoff.py sha256 19c94f7a…) | A six-item "critical" list was checked against the tree before anything was changed; **four were real and fixed, two were re-scoped**. *Real*: (3) `ReminderStore.update()`'s `mutate(items) or items` meant `cancel_reminder` returning `[]` was falsy, so **cancelling the last remaining reminder silently did nothing** — now a `None`-vs-list contract, both cases pinned. (4) the racy turn generation: the finding named `core/lifecycle.next_turn()`, which has **no runtime callers** — the eight live inline increments are now one locked `_bump_gen()`, and measurement corrected the finding (the plain one-liner did **not** reproduce: 64 000 claims, 0 duplicates under the GIL; the *wide* shape with `threading.Event()` built between the increment and the read produced **714 duplicates / 32 000 claims ≈ 2%**, which is the value the staleness checks and gen-keyed transcript cache trust). (5) `read_file` had no denylist **and `cat` is whitelisted**, so `run_command("cat ~/.ssh/id_rsa")` was a second route to the same content; one resolved-path predicate now covers `read_file`, `watch_file` and `run_command` args, refuses before the existence check (no oracle), and was tuned by measurement (`id_rsa*` wrongly refused `id_rsa_notes.md`). (6) the model-switch "memory cleared" only took a backup — and truncating the file is *not enough*, because the bubble holds `_history` in memory and rewrites it, so a `clear-history` socket action now tells the running bubble too; the same defect was sitting in the History tab's own Clear button (shared `_wipe_history`), and `reload_from_disk()` now rebases `_model_at_open` so an unrelated save after an external model change cannot destroy a conversation. *Re-scoped*: (1) the remote-Ollama opt-in disagreement is real in the predicate but **not reachable via `settings.json`** (every junk shape coerce-tested through the load path); it is now one `is True` predicate for guard and doctor alike, and doctor names the channel — which surfaced the genuinely invisible second opt-in, `HANDSOFF_ALLOW_REMOTE_OLLAMA` in the environment. (2) the control socket has no `SO_PEERCRED` check, but the proposed fix **cannot close the stated threat** (a same-uid process has our uid) — the real boundary, a `0700` state directory that `_prepare_runtime` refuses to start without, was already in place; a uid check was added as defence in depth with the limitation documented rather than papered over. Every guard was verified to fail on the mutated code. Deployed (`in-sync`, three-way TRUE, service active; the installed copies carry `denied_secret_path`, `_bump_gen`, `clear-history` and `_wipe_history`); 792 tests green, coverage 75.85% ≥ 70 (was 75.53%), `ci/compile_all.py` clean; human-only items unchanged |
| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (live redeploy) | uncommitted per-design-voice set (running == checkout == installed, handsoff.py sha256 9618bbe0…) | "Give each bubble design its own distinct voice reaction … instead of the shared uniform energy lift." The uniform lift — `energy * (1.0 + 0.45 * level)` folded once in `_frame()` — is gone; `_frame()` still publishes `level` and each painter writes its own term, so the twelve designs no longer react identically: orb 1351 changed px (ripple rings spreading through the glass), halo 4240 (torus thickens, wave + trailing bead, core flares), reactor 4396 (segments open, ticks extend, spin-up), bloom 7463 (petals open, extra sparks fly out), droplet 5226 (fast skin ripple, drip sooner), cube 6316 (a flash front sweeps the six facets), equalizer 2273 (the bars are the meter), crystal 6328 (inner hex swells off-phase and its hue splits away from the body — the refraction), saturn 1205 (ring thickens, wave travels it, moon pulled faster), void 1395 (infall faster, horizon flicker 13→24 Hz), sauron 5977 and pikachu 4996 (unchanged). The reactions differ in *where* they act too: the share of the change inside the inner half runs 21% (void, an outward event horizon) to 70% (saturn). Two invariants were measured rather than assumed. *Neutrality*: with the level pinned at 0, all twelve designs render pixel-identical to the previous tree — the comparison caught a real bug I had just written (the crystal's refracted pen colour was applied directly, so the *resting* gem took the state hue) and it now blends from white. *Footprint*: the first voice terms that add alpha to glows already reaching the window's round mask pushed bloom/cube/crystal from 0 to 461/175/313 px outside it — all faint (max alpha 32–38/255, all hidden by the mask) but ending on the cut at 22% opacity is a seam waiting to happen, so each pulls its gradient tail inward by `1.0 − 0.20 * level` (exactly 1.0 at silence) and all three are back to 0, with bloom's extra sparks capped at 0.95 R; the only remaining overflow is the pre-existing droplet (251 → 254 px) and saturn (6 → 8). The scenario now pins the crisp half of the regression — `_frame()`'s `energy` must be identical at level 0 and 1, so any re-introduced shared lift fails (verified) — and reconstructing the intermediate state (lift removed, no bespoke terms) makes it name exactly the five designs that relied on it: `orb=7, bloom=0, cube=0, crystal=0, saturn=0`. (2) The model-cache mirror could **undo a settings reload**: the push was read-then-assign, so a drop landing between the steps was overwritten by the stale model, which then *stayed* (the load saw a non-None cache, returned the old model, and the adopt republished it) — a voice or device change silently did nothing until the next reload. The push now reads **inside** the lock (my own first attempt read at the call site, which would have serialised the write while protecting nothing), the drop takes the same lock, and the adopt refuses to republish a model `core.audio` no longer holds. (3) The uninstaller `rm -rf`'d `~/.local/bin/core` unconditionally — a shared user directory, a plausible name for someone else's package, and the wipe ran even with **no manifest**, i.e. when nothing had named its contents; the directory is now dropped only when it is ours to drop, and the no-manifest fallback removes the checkout's own `core/*.py` so uninstall still cleans up. (4) `STAGE_DIR` was `staged.$$`, so the bubble's own self-edit restart racing a manual install would have both staging, gating and switching the **same** directory — now `mktemp -d`, with its parent created first. (5) Timed-out diagnostic workers cannot be cancelled, so one per request piled up against a wedged Ollama; at most one runs now, a second is refused by name, and the reply carries the cause instead of "see log". (6) A bounded `stop()` that gave up discarded the owner thread's late capture with it, so "push-to-talk did nothing" left no trace; it is now a WARNING with the frame count. *Disproved*: the ydotool probe does not false-positive — a socket inode with no listener returns **`ECONNREFUSED (111)` for both DGRAM and STREAM `connect()`** (measured), so a dead daemon cannot read as reachable; what remains (alive-but-wedged accepts, since the probe is a connect and not a round-trip) is stated as a documented limitation, because the proposed `/proc`-inode check was already measured to break a root-owned daemon. *Left alone*: the watcher's residual alternation ReDoS is bounded to one daemon thread and its cursor advance is bounded; it only matters if watcher patterns become user-facing free text. No test could see it: every fixture had been written in the parser's own quoting style. New `core/web.py` (application-free, host-injected like `core/bubble.py`) owns a **probed router** over keyless backends — local SearXNG (auto-detected, preferred, probed by a localhost connect), DuckDuckGo, Stack Exchange, Hacker News, GitHub, Wikipedia — plus a **page reader** that fetches locally and says so (`via local fetch`) and falls back to Jina only when the local fetch is useless, naming that too (`via Jina Reader (third-party)`). Backends are chosen by deterministic regexes over the query (errors → Stack Exchange, repos → GitHub, news → the general pair), a failing backend **falls through** to the next and EVERY failure is named in the answer (`nothing found — stackexchange (Stack Exchange): quota exhausted, ddg (DuckDuckGo): HTTP 503`), one in-flight request per backend is admitted through the shared `core/registry.py` helper, and a TTL cache means a repeated question asks nobody twice. One tool was added (48 total), not five, because the fixed prompt is the scarce resource: `web_search(source, read_top)` gains the two-stage find-then-READ path, `read_page` is the new one, and `_fixed_prompt_tokens` was measured (6327 of 32768, both web schemas 265 tokens). **Everything the survey ruled out is as much the finding as what it kept**: Jina's search endpoint is 401 without a token, Mojeek captcha-walls scripts, and a public SearXNG instance (searx.be) answers a non-browser with Cloudflare's "Verifying your browser…" — so keyless general search means a LOCAL instance or a scrape. Doctor gained `search:` and `reader:` lines that report what this process has OBSERVED and say `untried` for a backend nothing has asked — never `ok` because it is configured, which is the false-positive shape found in the reference project's own web channel (its `check()` does no request at all). **Two real defects, both found by the live proof and neither by reading the code**: (1) DuckDuckGo serves an anti-bot page (`anomaly.js`, `cc=botnet`) after a burst of queries from one address, and that was being reported as **"no results"** — a blocked search and an empty web are different facts, so it now raises a named failure the router records and the answer speaks, with all three outcomes (results / honest empty / not-a-result-page) pinned; (2) the reader's fallback rule was "local text under 200 characters", so `example.com` — sixty readable characters — was being sent to a third party for nothing; a page is judged useless locally only when the site blocked us or when a LOT of markup carries almost no text (a JavaScript shell). `example.com` now reads `via local fetch`. The reader also REFUSES loopback, link-local, private-range and `.local` addresses — including a public name that RESOLVES to one — so a pasted or injected URL cannot read the router's admin page or a cloud metadata endpoint into the transcript. Delegating `_ddg_lite` / `_wiki_search` to the new backend immediately caught a regression a guard was holding: the briefing's five-item news feed had been cut to four by the router's own limit. **35/35 mutations** back to the old behaviour caught (three of the first sweep's misses were weak GUARDS, not bad mutations — a challenge fixture short enough that the thin-page rule alone triggered the fallback, a resolver guard that never checked the searxng setting, and a `lookup_fact` fake that answered the endpoint which made the delegation untestable); **1181 tests green** in three orderings (default, shuffled-test seed 20260913, shuffled-file seed 7); coverage **81.49% ≥ 70** with `core/web.py` at **93%**; `ci/compile_all.py` clean (45 files); deployed `in-sync` (installed == checkout; `CORE_REQUIRED` and the rehearsal's declared set gained `web.py`) and verified against the DEPLOYED module: Stack Exchange 2 real hits (`quota 291/300`), DuckDuckGo 2 real hits with real URLs, `example.com` read locally, a Reddit thread named as refusing automated readers, `http://192.168.1.1/` refused, doctor printing `search: searxng not running, ddg ok (2s ago), stackexchange ok (3s ago, quota 291/300), hn untried, github untried, wikipedia untried` — the user's own appearance and settings left untouched. Two new control-socket verbs carry it: `preview-pack <folder>` and `preview-clear`, sent by the panel OFF the GUI thread (a socket round trip with a timeout must not freeze a window that may be trying to close). The expiry is checked on READ rather than by a timer, so the answer cannot be stale between the timer and the paint that follows it; a renewal already in flight can still land after a clear, which is bounded on purpose (the bubble drops what nobody renews) and so costs a few more seconds of the candidate rather than a stuck bubble, and the rest of the race is closed by the `_preview_art` guard — a reply that arrives after a preview was dropped writes nothing, so it cannot say "drawn on the bubble" about a candidate nothing has asked the bubble to draw yet. **The panel never claims more than it knows**: `drawn on the bubble` and `the bubble is not running, so only this window shows it` are different facts and telling them apart is why the command is sent at all rather than assumed; the clear is sent with its reply IGNORED (its note would belong to a preview that is already gone), and the window's close path now drops a preview whether or not it unpacked anything — a previewed FOLDER has no temporary folder, so the old `_preview_scratch` guard let it through. **`doctor` says it out loud**, because a preview draws art the settings do not name, which is exactly where the appearance line would otherwise describe a look the bubble is not drawing while someone stares at the difference: the line grows `— previewing Candidate (not installed)` and keeps describing the CONFIGURED look beside it. Both guards now stub an unflagged refusal (`no reminder matching 'tea' — it may have already been pruned`, `no weather provider is configured`) and go red on the reverted code. **11/11 mutations caught, zero residue** — the flag decoupled from the kind, the fail-closed fallback removed, the word boundary dropped, the dispatch classifying nothing, the gate refusal and the confirm/dry-run offers each losing their kind, the snooze clearing its offer on a refusal, the briefing reading a refusal as weather, dictation sniffing again and the notification reader sniffing again; every restore verified by sha256 (an earlier pass in this session destroyed a tracked file with a careless cleanup, so a restore is CHECKED, not assumed). The sweep is parsed with `ast`, not grepped, because the docstrings explaining the convention contain the same words and a text sweep would flag the explanation as the defect. **One self-inflicted failure, fixed at the cause:** adding `from core import tools` to three test files tripped the suite's own sandbox guard — a collection-time import bakes the developer's real `HOME`/XDG dirs into `core.tools` — so those files resolve the module lazily through `conftest.core_module("tools")`, the pattern the other suites already use (the guard was right). **P0-3 (startup in degraded modes) was verified already done, not re-done:** `_load_history` and `_load_memory` have guarded every failure path since the extraction, the `_take_missed_reminders()` startup call was wrapped in the previous pass (log, tell the person once, continue with `[]`), and both the unavailable and the healthy paths are pinned by tests in `tests/test_calendar.py`. **1380 tests green** in all three orderings (default, shuffled-test, shuffled-file); coverage **83.96% ≥ 70**; `compile`/`shell`/`smoke` clean — `bash ci/gates.sh` green gate by gate (761s). one that is neither fails; a listed name nothing reaches any more fails; and a listed name the module has since declared fails, because a paid debt left in the table is how a transition becomes a parking space. **Two names were API all along and lost the underscore** — `atomic_private_write` (four modules call it: host, settings app, the tool belt through its injected deps, and the tests) and `MIC_OPERATION_LOCK` (the app-wide PortAudio lock the host takes around stream construction and teardown) — 35 sites, mechanical, no fallout. **`core/calendar.py` had five private names reached and ZERO public ones**, which is the clearest case of a boundary that was only a naming convention: `ics_fetch`, `ics_events_from_text`, `fmt_events`, `DAY_NAMES`, `MONTH_NAMES` are its interface now (28 sites), and the map's row names them. **Eleven were NOT renamed and are declared as debt instead**, with the reason per module: `core/settings.py`'s eight are steps of ONE lifecycle (load → drop retired keys → migrate → write under a file lock, backup, quarantine), where the fix is the module owning the sequence rather than eight renames — and `_load_settings` alone has 37 sites, `_DEFAULT_DEPS` 30, most of them `monkeypatch.setattr` string names. `core/tools.py`'s two are injected STATE, not functions: nothing to promote, only something to stop reaching for. `core/brain.py`'s one belongs on whatever both callers should share. **Two measurements were wrong before they were right, and both were caught by the guard rather than by review:** the alias map knew only `load_module`, so the whole `_load_module("x")` handle family — and `_core_settings = _load_core_package()`, the settings module's own loader, because it loads before the core package exists — was invisible; with all three forms the crossing set went from 8 names to 22 across 5 modules. And attributing `call()._x` by name-uniqueness reported twelve false positives out of `core/tools.py` alone (`_dep()._geocode`, `_dep()._mpc` are the HOST's namespace through the injected dependency object, not another module's), so that branch is now only `_core_module("literal")`. **8/8 mutations caught across two sweeps, zero residue, every restore sha256-verified**, plus two probes confirming the sanctioned paths are open (a declared seam crossed on purpose, and a promoted name that no longer crosses). 1452 tests green, `compile`/`shell`/`smoke` PASS. **Shipped bytes changed, so the deployment is behind this checkout until redeployed** — done in the same pass, verified by 17/17 byte-identical files and `deployment: in-sync` |
